Each invocation resolves the database, runs exactly one command, writes CSV to standard output on success or one diagnostic line to standard error on failure, and exits with a code from the exit-code table. Every command checks all inputs and rules before its first write.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|------------|------------------|\n| Python 3.11+ | All application code, with the project tree, `.venv` (created by `uv venv`), `pyproject.toml` and committed `uv.lock`. |\n| SQLite | The store, reached only through the generated `persistence` package. A `postgres://` or `postgresql://` DSN selects PostgreSQL through the same package. |\n| CLI | The `ledger` executable at the application root, dispatching to `cli.main`. |\n| pytest | The test runner behind `bin/test.sh`. |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh`, the `ledger` launcher and the supplied grader. |\n\n## Modules\n\n| Module | Responsibility |\n|--------|----------------|\n| `ledger` (executable, POSIX sh) | Launcher: picks `.venv/bin/python` when present, else `python3`, sets `PYTHONPATH` to the application root and runs `python -m cli`. |\n| `cli/__init__.py` | `main(argv) -> int`, the command registry `register_command(name, handler)`, dispatch. |\n| `cli/__main__.py` | Calls `sys.exit(main())`. |\n| `cli/testing.py` | `cli_runner(*args) -> (exit_code, stdout, stderr)`. |\n| `ledger_config.py` | `database_dsn(env=None)` and `open_db()`. |\n| `ledger_errors.py` | Error types, exit codes and the single-line stderr handler. |\n| `csv_output.py` | `format_cents` and `write_rows`. |\n| `validation.py` | Code, text, amount and date validation. |\n| `balance_rules.py` | Final-balance check shared by every writing command. |\n| `commands/` | One module per command family, each registering its handlers. |\n| `persistence/` | Generated by Drydock from `DATABASE.md`; never hand-written. |\n\n## Module Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning module | Low-level APIs it may access |\n|----------|---------------|------------------------------|\n| Persistence | `persistence/` (generated) | The database driver, SQL, sessions. |\n| Configuration | `ledger_config.py` | `os.environ`, `persistence.open_database`. |\n| CLI | `cli/` | `sys.argv`, `sys.stdout`, `sys.stderr`. |\n| Diagnostics | `ledger_errors.py` | `sys.stderr`. |\n| File reading | `commands/load*.py` | Opening the CSV path given on the command line. |\n\nNo other module imports `sqlite3`, `psycopg` or any other database driver.\n\n## Command Handler Contract\n\nA handler is `handler(args: list[str]) -> None`, where `args` are the arguments after the command name. A handler validates everything, computes its result, and only then writes to the database and to standard output. It signals failure by raising a `ledger_errors` exception and never writes to standard output before it can no longer fail.\n\n## Commands\n\n`init`, `count`, `load accounts|categories|transactions`, `account add|get|rename|delete`, `txn post|get|amend|delete`, `transfer`, `report balances|categories|monthly`.\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|------|---------|\n| 0 | Success. |\n| 1 | Operational failure: database unreachable, tables missing, file unreadable, unexpected error. |\n| 2 | Usage error: unknown command, table or report, wrong argument count. |\n| 3 | Rejected: invalid input, duplicate key, unknown reference, account in use, negative balance. |\n| 4 | Not found. |\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Money is integer cents at every layer; no floating point anywhere.\n- Report values are always computed from stored rows; none is embedded.\n- A rejected command changes nothing: validation and the balance rule run before the first write, and writes for one command share one database transaction.\n- No application-level authentication; diagnostics never echo a DSN or credential.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC foundation-project-tree ===\nIntent: The Python project files and the persistence package exist and the interpreter meets the minimum version.\n\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert sys.version_info >= (3, 11)\nassert Path(\"pyproject.toml\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"uv.lock\").is_file()\nimport persistence\nassert callable(persistence.open_database)\n=== END AC foundation-project-tree ===\n\n=== AC foundation-persistence-opens ===\nIntent: The generated persistence package opens the configured store and exposes the three declared tables.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    for table in (\"accounts\", \"categories\", \"transactions\"):\n        assert hasattr(db, table)\n    assert isinstance(db.accounts.list(), list)\n=== END AC foundation-persistence-opens ===\n\n=== AC foundation-no-direct-drivers ===\nIntent: No application module outside the persistence package imports a database driver.\n\nimport ast\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nskip = {\".venv\", \"persistence\", \"tests\", \"sources\", \"__pycache__\", \".git\"}\ndrivers = {\"sqlite3\", \"psycopg\", \"psycopg2\", \"sqlalchemy\"}\noffenders = []\nfor path in Path(\".\").rglob(\"*.py\"):\n    if skip & set(path.parts):\n        continue\n    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):\n            names = {a.name.split(\".\")[0] for a in node.names}\n        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):\n            names = {(node.module or \"\").split(\".\")[0]}\n        else:\n            continue\n        if names & drivers:\n            offenders.append(str(path))\nprint(offenders)\nassert offenders == []\n=== END AC foundation-no-direct-drivers ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Application code never opens a database driver; all access goes through the generated persistence package.\n- No floating point for money at any layer.\n- Do not modify anything under `sources/`, and never read `sources/run.sh` or a seed file from application code.\n- No report row, count or total is hardcoded.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CliFoundation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Cli Foundation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | The executable `ledger`, its command registry and dispatcher, and database location resolution. |\n| Depends On | ARCHITECTURE.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | ledger, ledger.register_command, ledger_config.database_dsn |\n| Consumes | persistence.open_database |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nGive every later command a place to register and a single way to resolve and open the database.\n\n## Executable\n\n`ledger` sits at the application root with its executable bit set. It is a POSIX sh script that resolves its own directory, chooses `.venv/bin/python` when present and `python3` otherwise, and runs `PYTHONPATH=<root> exec <python> -m cli \"$@\"`. It does not change the working directory, so `data/app.db` is relative to the caller.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int` uses `sys.argv[1:]` when `argv` is `None`.\n- `cli.register_command(name: str, handler)` registers a handler under `name`, either one word (`init`) or two words (`account add`).\n- Dispatch: when at least two arguments are given and `args[0] + \" \" + args[1]` is registered, that handler receives `args[2:]`; otherwise when `args[0]` is registered its handler receives `args[1:]`. An empty argument list or an unregistered command writes one line to standard error and returns `2`, with nothing on standard output.\n- A handler that returns normally yields exit `0`. Before the error-contract story lands, any exception from a handler writes one generic line to standard error and returns `1`.\n- `cli/testing.py` provides `cli_runner(*args) -> (exit_code, stdout, stderr)` as in the CLI foundation contract.\n\n## Database Location\n\n`ledger_config.database_dsn(env=None) -> str` reads `env` (default `os.environ`): `DATABASE_URL` when set and non-empty, else `DATABASE_PATH` when set and non-empty, else `data/app.db`. The value is returned unchanged. `ledger_config.open_db()` returns `persistence.open_database(database_dsn())`. The DSN is never printed.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cli-executable-present ===\nIntent: The ledger launcher exists at the application root, is executable and starts with a shebang.\n\nimport os\n\nassert os.path.isfile(\"ledger\")\nassert os.access(\"ledger\", os.X_OK)\nwith open(\"ledger\", \"r\", encoding=\"ascii\") as handle:\n    first = handle.read(2)\nassert first == \"#!\"\n=== END AC cli-executable-present ===\n\n=== AC cli-no-arguments-usage ===\nIntent: Calling main with no arguments Cells that are `int` are rendered with `str`; a `float` cell raises `TypeError`. Nothing is written when a row is invalid.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC csv-format-cents-values ===\nIntent: format_cents renders zero, positive, negative and large amounts with two decimals ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ErrorContract.md ===\n# FEATURE: Error Contract\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Error types, exit codes and the standard output stays empty.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `ledger_errors.LedgerError(Exception)` is the base, with class attribute `exit_code`.\n- `Rejected` has `exit_code` 3, `NotFound` 4, `OperationalFailure` 1, `UsageError` 2.\n- `ledger_errors.handle(exc: BaseException) -> int` writes one line to standard error and returns the exit code: the `exit_code` of a `LedgerError`, otherwise `1` (unexpected errors, including database errors). The message has newlines collapsed to spaces and every `scheme://...` token replaced by `<redacted>`, so a DSN or password never reaches a diagnostic.\n- `cli.main` calls `handle` for any exception raised by a handler. An unknown command or empty argument list is a `UsageError` and exits 2.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC errors-exit-codes ===\nIntent: Each error type carries the exit code from the contract  ledger_errors.NotFound,\n):\n    assert issubclass(cls, ledger_errors.LedgerError)\n=== END AC errors-exit-codes ===\n\n=== AC errors-through-main ===\nIntent: A handler raising each error type makes END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Validation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Validation of codes, names, Names and descriptions use the defaults; memos use `max_len=60, min_len=0`. Returns the value.\n- `parse_amount(text: str) -> int`: the whole string matches `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]{1,2})?` (ASCII digits). No leading dot, plus sign, spaces, thousands separator, trailing dot or more than two decimals. The result is integer cents computed with integer arithmetic only, and a zero amount in any form (`0`, `0.00`, `-0`) is rejected.\n- `parse_date(text: str) -> str`: matches `YYYY-MM-DD` with ASCII digits and is a real calendar date, with leap years honored (`2028-02-29` valid, `2026-02-29` invalid). Returns the text unchanged.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC validation-amount-valid ===\nIntent: parse_amount converts valid amounts to integer cents.\n\nfrom validation import accepted: \" + repr(text))\n=== END AC validation-amount-rejected ===\n\n=== AC validation-date-valid ===\nIntent: parse_date accepts real calendar dates including a leap day in a leap year.\n\nfrom BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: LayersTesting\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, boundaries and technical decisions for the LayersTesting command-line cash ledger. |\n| Provides | python project tree |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`ledger` is a single-operator command-line cash ledger. Each invocation resolves the database, runs exactly one command, writes CSV to standard output on success or one diagnostic line to standard error on failure, and exits with a code from the exit-code table. Every command checks all inputs and rules before its first write.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`; that file remains the decision of record.\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|------------|------------------|\n| Python 3.11+ | All application code, with the project tree, `.venv` (created by `uv venv`), `pyproject.toml` and committed `uv.lock`. |\n| SQLite | The store, reached only through the generated `persistence` package. A `postgres://` or `postgresql://` DSN selects PostgreSQL through the same package. |\n| CLI | The `ledger` executable at the application root, dispatching to `cli.main`. |\n| pytest | The test runner behind `bin/test.sh`. |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh`, the `ledger` launcher and the supplied grader. |\n\n## Modules\n\n| Module | Responsibility |\n|--------|----------------|\n| `ledger` (executable, POSIX sh) | Launcher: picks `.venv/bin/python` when present, else `python3`, sets `PYTHONPATH` to the application root and runs `python -m cli`. |\n| `cli/__init__.py` | `main(argv) -> int`, the command registry `register_command(name, handler)`, dispatch. |\n| `cli/__main__.py` | Calls `sys.exit(main())`. |\n| `cli/testing.py` | `cli_runner(*args) -> (exit_code, stdout, stderr)`. |\n| `ledger_config.py` | `database_dsn(env=None)` and `open_db()`. |\n| `ledger_errors.py` | Error types, exit codes and the single-line stderr handler. |\n| `csv_output.py` | `format_cents` and `write_rows`. |\n| `validation.py` | Code, text, amount and date validation. |\n| `balance_rules.py` | Final-balance check shared by every writing command. |\n| `commands/` | One module per command family, each registering its handlers. |\n| `persistence/` | Generated by Drydock from `DATABASE.md`; never hand-written. |\n\n## Module Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning module | Low-level APIs it may access |\n|----------|---------------|------------------------------|\n| Persistence | `persistence/` (generated) | The database driver, SQL, sessions. |\n| Configuration | `ledger_config.py` | `os.environ`, `persistence.open_database`. |\n| CLI | `cli/` | `sys.argv`, `sys.stdout`, `sys.stderr`. |\n| Diagnostics | `ledger_errors.py` | `sys.stderr`. |\n| File reading | `commands/load*.py` | Opening the CSV path given on the command line. |\n\nNo other module imports `sqlite3`, `psycopg` or any other database driver.\n\n## Command Handler Contract\n\nA handler is `handler(args: list[str]) -> None`, where `args` are the arguments after the command name. A handler validates everything, computes its result, and only then writes to the database and to standard output. It signals failure by raising a `ledger_errors` exception and never writes to standard output before it can no longer fail.\n\n## Commands\n\n`init`, `count`, `load accounts|categories|transactions`, `account add|get|rename|delete`, `txn post|get|amend|delete`, `transfer`, `report balances|categories|monthly`.\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|------|---------|\n| 0 | Success. |\n| 1 | Operational failure: database unreachable, tables missing, file unreadable, unexpected error. |\n| 2 | Usage error: unknown command, table or report, wrong argument count. |\n| 3 | Rejected: invalid input, duplicate key, unknown reference, account in use, negative balance. |\n| 4 | Not found. |\n\n## Technical Decisions\n\n- Money is integer cents at every layer; no floating point anywhere.\n- Report values are always computed from stored rows; none is embedded.\n- A rejected command changes nothing: validation and the balance rule run before the first write, and writes for one command share one database transaction.\n- No application-level authentication; diagnostics never echo a DSN or credential.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC foundation-project-tree ===\nIntent: The Python project files and the persistence package exist and the interpreter meets the minimum version.\n\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert sys.version_info >= (3, 11)\nassert Path(\"pyproject.toml\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"uv.lock\").is_file()\nimport persistence\nassert callable(persistence.open_database)\n=== END AC foundation-project-tree ===\n\n=== AC foundation-persistence-opens ===\nIntent: The generated persistence package opens the configured store and exposes the three declared tables.\n\nfrom persistence.testing import db_session\n\nwith db_session() as db:\n    for table in (\"accounts\", \"categories\", \"transactions\"):\n        assert hasattr(db, table)\n    assert isinstance(db.accounts.list(), list)\n=== END AC foundation-persistence-opens ===\n\n=== AC foundation-no-direct-drivers ===\nIntent: No application module outside the persistence package imports a database driver.\n\nimport ast\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nskip = {\".venv\", \"persistence\", \"tests\", \"sources\", \"__pycache__\", \".git\"}\ndrivers = {\"sqlite3\", \"psycopg\", \"psycopg2\", \"sqlalchemy\"}\noffenders = []\nfor path in Path(\".\").rglob(\"*.py\"):\n    if skip & set(path.parts):\n        continue\n    tree = ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):\n            names = {a.name.split(\".\")[0] for a in node.names}\n        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):\n            names = {(node.module or \"\").split(\".\")[0]}\n        else:\n            continue\n        if names & drivers:\n            offenders.append(str(path))\nprint(offenders)\nassert offenders == []\n=== END AC foundation-no-direct-drivers ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Application code never opens a database driver; all access goes through the generated persistence package.\n- No floating point for money at any layer.\n- Do not modify anything under `sources/`, and never read `sources/run.sh` or a seed file from application code.\n- No report row, count or total is hardcoded.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CliFoundation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Cli Foundation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | The executable `ledger`, its command registry and dispatcher, and database location resolution. |\n| Depends On | ARCHITECTURE.md, DATABASE.md |\n| Provides | ledger, ledger.register_command, ledger_config.database_dsn |\n| Consumes | persistence.open_database |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nGive every later command a place to register and a single way to resolve and open the database.\n\n## Executable\n\n`ledger` sits at the application root with its executable bit set. It is a POSIX sh script that resolves its own directory, chooses `.venv/bin/python` when present and `python3` otherwise, and runs `PYTHONPATH=<root> exec <python> -m cli \"$@\"`. It does not change the working directory, so `data/app.db` is relative to the caller.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `cli.main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int` uses `sys.argv[1:]` when `argv` is `None`.\n- `cli.register_command(name: str, handler)` registers a handler under `name`, either one word (`init`) or two words (`account add`).\n- Dispatch: when at least two arguments are given and `args[0] + \" \" + args[1]` is registered, that handler receives `args[2:]`; otherwise when `args[0]` is registered its handler receives `args[1:]`. An empty argument list or an unregistered command writes one line to standard error and returns `2`, with nothing on standard output.\n- A handler that returns normally yields exit `0`. Before the error-contract story lands, any exception from a handler writes one generic line to standard error and returns `1`.\n- `cli/testing.py` provides `cli_runner(*args) -> (exit_code, stdout, stderr)` as in the CLI foundation contract.\n\n## Database Location\n\n`ledger_config.database_dsn(env=None) -> str` reads `env` (default `os.environ`): `DATABASE_URL` when set and non-empty, else `DATABASE_PATH` when set and non-empty, else `data/app.db`. The value is returned unchanged. `ledger_config.open_db()` returns `persistence.open_database(database_dsn())`. The DSN is never printed.\n\n## Database Interface\n\n- None — persistence is owned by DATABASE.md.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cli-executable-present ===\nIntent: The ledger launcher exists at the application root, is executable and starts with a shebang.\n\nimport os\n\nassert os.path.isfile(\"ledger\")\nassert os.access(\"ledger\", os.X_OK)\nwith open(\"ledger\", \"r\", encoding=\"ascii\") as handle:\n    first = handle.read(2)\nassert first == \"#!\"\n=== END AC cli-executable-present ===\n\n=== AC cli-no-arguments-usage ===\nIntent: Calling main with no arguments exits 2 and writes nothing to standard output.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner()\nprint(err)\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC cli-no-arguments-usage ===\n\n=== AC cli-unknown-command ===\nIntent: An unregistered command exits 2 with nothing on standard output.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"zzunknowncmd\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\n=== END AC cli-unknown-command ===\n\n=== AC cli-register-one-word ===\nIntent: A registered one-word command receives the remaining \"beta\"]\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(name, *given)\nassert code == 0\nassert seen == [given]\n=== END AC cli-register-one-word ===\n\n=== AC cli-register-two-word ===\nIntent: A registered two-word command is selected over the one-word group and receives the remaining arguments.\n\nimport cli\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ngroup = \"zzgroup\"\nverb = \"sub\"\nseen = []\ncli.register_command(group + \" \" + verb, lambda args: seen.append(list(args)))\ngiven = [\"x\"]\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(group, verb, *given)\nassert code == 0\nassert seen == [given]\n=== result.stdout == \"\"\n=== END AC cli-launcher-no-arguments ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- The DSN, any path credential and any password are never written to standard output or standard error.\n- The database is opened only through `persistence.open_database`; no driver is imported.\n- A dispatch failure writes nothing to standard output and nothing to the database.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CsvOutput.md ===\n# FEATURE: Csv Output\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Integer-only cents formatting and CSV row writing to standard output. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md |\n| Provides | csv_output.format_cents, csv_output.write_rows |\n| Consumes | ledger.register_command |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery successful command writes its result through this module so that formatting is identical everywhere.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `csv_output.format_cents(cents: int) -> str` returns the amount with exactly two decimals, a leading `-` when negative, no thousands separator, and `0.00` for zero (never `-0.00`). Computed with integer arithmetic (`divmod` on the absolute value). A non-`int` argument (including `float` and `bool`) raises `TypeError`.\n- `csv_output.write_rows(rows) -> None` writes each row to the current `sys.stdout` (looked up at call time) as its cells joined by `,`, with no quoting, no trailing spaces and a `\\n` line ending. Cells that are `int` are rendered with `str`; a `float` cell raises `TypeError`. Nothing is written when a row is invalid.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC csv-format-cents-values ===\nIntent: format_cents renders zero, positive, negative and large amounts with two decimals and no separator.\n\nfrom csv_output import format_cents\n\nassert format_cents(0) == \"0.00\"\nassert format_cents(5) == \"0.05\"\nassert format_cents(-5) == \"-0.05\"\nassert format_cents(-1250) == \"-12.50\"\nassert format_cents(1250) == \"12.50\"\nassert format_cents(500000) == \"5000.00\"\nassert format_cents(123456789) == \"1234567.89\"\n=== END AC csv-format-cents-values ===\n\n=== AC \"100\", None):\n    try:\n        format_cents(bad)\n    except TypeError:\n        continue\n    raise AssertionError(\"format_cents accepted a non-int value\")\n=== END AC ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ErrorContract.md ===\n# FEATURE: Error Contract\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Error types, exit codes and the one-line standard error diagnostic for failed commands. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-CliFoundation.md |\n| Provides | ledger_errors.Rejected, ledger_errors.NotFound, ledger_errors.OperationalFailure, ledger_errors.UsageError |\n| Consumes | ledger.register_command |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nFailures map to a fixed exit code and exactly one diagnostic line on standard error; standard output stays empty.\n\n## Interface\n\n- `ledger_errors.LedgerError(Exception)` is the base, with class attribute `exit_code`.\n- `Rejected` has `exit_code` 3, `NotFound` 4, `OperationalFailure` 1, `UsageError` 2.\n- `ledger_errors.handle(exc: BaseException) -> int` writes one line to standard error and returns the exit code: the `exit_code` of a `LedgerError`, otherwise `1` (unexpected errors, including database errors). The message has newlines collapsed to spaces and every `scheme://...` token replaced by `<redacted>`, so a DSN or password never reaches a diagnostic.\n- `cli.main` calls `handle` for any exception raised by a handler. An unknown command or empty argument list is a `UsageError` and exits 2.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC errors-exit-codes ===\nIntent: Each error type carries the exit code from the contract and derives from the common base.\n\nimport ledger_errors\n\nassert ledger_errors.OperationalFailure.exit_code == 1\nassert ledger_errors.UsageError.exit_code == 2\nassert ledger_errors.Rejected.exit_code == 3\nassert ledger_errors.NotFound.exit_code == 4\nfor cls in (\n    ledger_errors.OperationalFailure,\n    ledger_errors.UsageError,\n    ledger_errors.Rejected,\n    ledger_errors.NotFound,\n):\n    assert issubclass(cls, ledger_errors.LedgerError)\n=== END AC errors-exit-codes ===\n\n=== AC errors-through-main ===\nIntent: A handler raising each error type makes main return its exit code with empty standard output and one stderr line.\n\nimport cli\nimport ledger_errors\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncases = {\n    \"zzrej\": (ledger_errors.Rejected, 3),\n    \"zznf\": (ledger_errors.NotFound, 4),\n    \"zzop\": (ledger_errors.OperationalFailure, 1),\n    \"zzuse\": (ledger_errors.UsageError, 2),\n}\nfor name, (cls, expected_code) in cases.items():\n    def handler(args, cls=cls):\n        raise cls(\"diagnostic text\")\n    cli.register_command(name, handler)\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(name)\n    assert code == expected_code\n    assert out == \"\"\n    assert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC errors-through-main ===\n\n=== AC errors-unexpected-exception ===\nIntent: An unexpected exception from a handler exits 1 with empty standard output.\n\nimport cli\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nname = \"zzboom\"\ndef handler(args):\n    raise RuntimeError(\"unexpected\")\ncli.register_command(name, handler)\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(name)\nassert code == 1\nassert out == \"\"\nassert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC errors-unexpected-exception ===\n\n=== AC errors-unknown-command-usage ===\nIntent: An unknown command is a usage error: exit 2, empty standard output, one diagnostic line.\n\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"zzdoesnotexist\")\nassert code == 2\nassert out == \"\"\nassert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC errors-unknown-command-usage ===\n\n=== AC errors-multiline-message-single-line ===\nIntent: A multi-line error message is still written as exactly one diagnostic line.\n\nimport cli\nimport ledger_errors\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nname = \"zzmulti\"\nmessage = \"first line\\nsecond line\"\ndef handler(args):\n    raise ledger_errors.Rejected(message)\ncli.register_command(name, handler)\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(name)\nassert code == 3\nassert len(err.splitlines()) == 1\n=== END AC errors-multiline-message-single-line ===\n\n=== AC errors-dsn-never-echoed ===\nIntent: A password inside a DSN in an error message never appears on standard error or standard output.\n\nimport cli\nimport ledger_errors\nfrom cli.testing import cli_runner\n\nname = \"zzsecret\"\npassword = \"s3cretpw\"\nmessage = \"cannot open postgres://user:\" + password + \"@host/db\"\ndef handler(args):\n    raise ledger_errors.OperationalFailure(message)\ncli.register_command(name, handler)\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(name)\nassert code == 1\nassert password not in err\nassert password not in out\n=== END AC errors-dsn-never-echoed ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- A failing command writes nothing to standard output.\n- A diagnostic never contains a database URL, path credential or DSN password.\n- Diagnostic text is never part of the contract; only the exit code is.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-Validation.md ===\n# FEATURE: Validation\n\n| Field | Value |\n|-------|-------|\n| Version | 20261026 V1 |\n| Description | Validation of codes, names, memos, amounts and dates, with amounts converted to integer cents. |\n| Depends On | FEATURE-ErrorContract.md |\n| Provides | validation.parse_amount, validation.parse_date, validation.check_code, validation.check_text |\n| Consumes | ledger_errors.Rejected |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nEvery command validates its inputs through this module before it writes. Every violation raises `ledger_errors.Rejected` (exit 3).\n\n## Interface\n\n- `check_code(value: str) -> str`: 1 to 12 characters, each in `A-Z` or `0-9` (ASCII only). Returns the value.\n- `check_text(value: str, max_len: int = 40, min_len: int = 1) -> str`: length within `min_len..max_len`, no comma, no newline or carriage return. Names and descriptions use the defaults; memos use `max_len=60, min_len=0`. Returns the value.\n- `parse_amount(text: str) -> int`: the whole string matches `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]{1,2})?` (ASCII digits). No leading dot, plus sign, spaces, thousands separator, trailing dot or more than two decimals. The result is integer cents computed with integer arithmetic only, and a zero amount in any form (`0`, `0.00`, `-0`) is rejected.\n- `parse_date(text: str) -> str`: matches `YYYY-MM-DD` with ASCII digits and is a real calendar date, with leap years honored (`2028-02-29` valid, `2026-02-29` invalid). Returns the text unchanged.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC validation-amount-valid ===\nIntent: parse_amount converts valid amounts to integer cents.\n\nfrom validation import parse_amount\n\nassert parse_amount(\"12.50\") == 1250\nassert parse_amount(\"-12.5\") == -1250\nassert parse_amount(\"5\") == 500\nassert parse_amount(\"-0.01\") == -1\nassert parse_amount(\"0.10\") == 10\nassert parse_amount(\"5000.00\") == 500000\nassert isinstance(parse_amount(\"1.00\"), int)\n=== END AC validation-amount-valid ===\n\n=== AC validation-amount-rejected ===\nIntent: parse_amount rejects zero in every form and every malformed amount.\n\nfrom ledger_errors import Rejected\nfrom validation import parse_amount\n\nbad = [\"0\", \"0.00\", \"-0\", \"-0.00\", \".50\", \"+1\", \" 1\", \"1 \", \"1,000\", \"1.234\", \"1.\", \"\", \"-\", \"abc\", \"1e3\"]\nfor text in bad:\n    try:\n        parse_amount(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        continue\n    raise AssertionError(\"amount accepted: \" + repr(text))\n=== END AC validation-amount-rejected ===\n\n=== AC validation-date-valid ===\nIntent: parse_date accepts real calendar dates including a leap day in a leap year.\n\nfrom validation import parse_date\n\nfor text in (\"2026-01-01\", \"2026-04-30\", \"2028-02-29\", \"2026-12-31\"):\n    assert parse_date(text) == text\n=== END AC validation-date-valid ===\n\n=== AC validation-date-rejected ===\nIntent: parse_date rejects impossible dates and malformed spellings.\n\nfrom ledger_errors import Rejected\nfrom validation import parse_date\n\nbad = [\"2026-02-29\", \"2026-02-30\", \"2026-04-31\", \"2026-13-01\", \"2026-00-10\", \"2026-1-01\", \"20260101\", \"2026/01/01\", \"\", \"2026-01-1x\"]\nfor text in bad:\n    try:\n        parse_date(text)\n    except Rejected:\n        continue\n    raise AssertionError(\"date accepted: \" + repr(text))\n=== END AC validation-date-rejected ===\n\n=== AC validation-code ===\nIntent: check_code accepts 1 to 12 characters of A-Z and 0-9 and rejects everything else.\n\nfrom ledger_errors import Rejected\nfrom validation import check_code\n\ngood = [\"A\", \"DRAWER1\", \"A\" * 12, \"0123456789AB\"]\nfor value in good:\n    assert check_code(value) == value\nbad = [\"\", \"A\" * 13, \"abc\", \"A-B\", \"A B\", \"A,B\", \"SAFE\\n\"]\nfor value in bad:\n    try:\n        check_code(value)\n    except Rejected:\n        continue\n    raise AssertionError(\"code accepted: \" + repr(value))\n=== END AC validation-code ===\n\n=== AC validation-text-name ===\nIntent: check_text enforces 1 to 40 characters with no comma or newline by default.\n\nfrom ledger_errors import Rejected\nfrom validation import check_text\n\ngood = [\"Front Drawer\", \"x\", \"n\" * 40]\nfor value in good:\n    assert check_text(value) == value\nbad = [\"\", \"n\" * 41, \"a,b\", \"line1\\nline2\", \"line1\\rline2\"]\nfor value in bad:\n    try:\n        check_text(value)\n    except Rejected:\n        continue\n    raise AssertionError(\"text accepted: \" + repr(value))\n=== END AC validation-text-name ===\n\n=== AC validation-text-memo ===\nIntent: check_text with memo limits allows an empty memo up to 60 characters and rejects longer or comma-bearing memos.\n\nfrom ledger_errors import Rejected\nfrom validation import check_text\n\nempty = \"\"\nassert check_text(empty, max_len=60, min_len=0) == empty\nfull = \"m\" * 60\nassert check_text(full, max_len=60, min_len=0) == full\nfor value in (\"m\" * 61, \"a,b\", \"a\\nb\"):\n    try:\n        check_text(value, max_len=60, min_len=0)\n    except Rejected:\n        continue\n    raise AssertionError(\"memo accepted: \" + repr(value))\n=== END AC validation-text-memo ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Amounts are parsed with integer arithmetic only; no floating point is used.\n- Validation raises `ledger_errors.Rejected`; it never writes to the database or to standard output.\n- Only ASCII digits and letters are accepted in codes, amounts and dates.\n=== END ARTIFACT
